Session Handoff — Conditional Bootcamp: exact drill grading, "rule" copy pass, new indicator-word flashcards
Where it started
The session picked up the Conditional Bootcamp handoff, with part 1 of deterministic grading approved and a "sentence" → "rule" copy pass pending. Midway, Joshua also approved part 2, the AI judge. Later he redirected to the indicator flashcards: a word-first deck where the student commits to Group 1 or Group 2, the card flips, and the back says the group. The flashcard design came from a `/grill-me` interview. Constraints held throughout: never commit to main, no Lovable credit spend without confirmation right before the action, push only your own commits.
Decisions locked + what shipped
Branch `claude/conditional-bootcamp-drills-2026-10-11`, PR https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/pull/204 (repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`). Nothing is merged or deployed. All three commits are pushed.

* `0e2b56e5`: exact grading in the worksheet drills.
   * Conditions: the student highlights each condition in the rule (first and last word), checked against hand-authored spans for 83 rules in `...\content\conditionSpans.ts`. A dropped article or modal at the edge is accepted.
   * Symbols: one or two distinct letters.
   * Diagram and contrapositive: built from the student's own letters in left/right slots with NOT toggles, checked exactly. The engine is `...\components\SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx`.
   * Walkthroughs and set instructions: updated in `...\content\drillDemos.ts` and `sourceWorksheetDrills.ts`.
   * CSS fix: selected choice buttons had white text on pale green, now fixed.
* `9657b2ee`: "sentence" → "rule" across lesson copy.
   * Scope: about 294 swaps of "the/this/that/each/whole sentence", plus tests.
   * Bundle: regenerated `src\bootcamps\conditionals\data\curriculum.generated.json` with `npm run content:conditionals`, because a parity test compares it to `foundations.source.ts`.
   * Left alone: generic uses such as "original sentence" and "a different sentence", and the "if/then sentence" the student writes.
* `bbf844ff`: indicator flashcards.
   * Deck: one mixed deck of 20 word cards. Group 1 has 13: if, when, whenever, where, all, every, any, anyone, anytime, every time, each, the only, who. Group 2 has 7: only, only if, only when, only where, always, must, requires. "Only" and "the only" are separate cards.
   * Round: a miss shakes the card only, then flips and corrects. Missed cards return once at the end.
   * Saving: progress saves to the account `course` record under step id `flashcards-indicators` (device `localStorage` fallback key `conditional-indicator-words-v1`).
   * Placement: the deck opens only once the work includes both groups. The Group 2 lesson hosts it. The Group 1 lesson gets a one-line pointer. This was my call, not Joshua's.
   * Kept as they were: the 4 concept cards and the old question-card data.
   * Files:
      * `...\content\indicatorWordCards.ts`
      * `...\indicatorWordDeck.ts`
      * `...\components\IndicatorWordDeck.tsx`
      * `...\components\indicator-word-deck.css`
      * `...\components\FlashcardTools.tsx`
* Part 2 (AI judge) is not built.
   * Why it's blocked: the database allows only `assignment` and `preview` source kinds (`...\supabase\migrations\20261001120000_activity_ai_admission_v1.sql`), so a student can't request a judgment on a bootcamp drill.
   * Fix I proposed: a third `bootcamp` source kind, which needs a migration, an `activity-coach` change and a Lovable deploy.
   * Status: Joshua redirected to flashcards before answering. His earlier "yes to both" was about the lesson swap and approving part 2 in principle. The deploy still needs confirmation immediately before it.

export const INDICATOR_DECK_STEP = 'flashcards-indicators';
const DEVICE_KEY = 'conditional-indicator-words-v1';

function readDevice(): unknown {
  try { return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(DEVICE_KEY) ?? 'null'); } catch { return null; }
}

/** One word per card, both groups mixed. The student commits to a group, the card flips, and the back says which group it was. */
export function IndicatorWordDeck({ stepId = INDICATOR_DECK_STEP }: { stepId?: string }) {
  const course = useCourseStep(stepId);
  const record = course?.record;
  const [state, setState] = useState<DeckState>(() => readDeckState(course?.saved?.state) ?? readDeckState(readDevice()) ?? newRound());
  const next = useRef<HTMLButtonElement>(null);
  const heading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);
  const finished = isFinished(state);
  const touched = state.picked !== null || state.index > 0 || state.round > 1;

  // Every answer is saved to the account, so the student can leave and pick up on the same card in the same order.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (touched) record?.({ state, done: Math.min(state.index + (state.picked !== null ? 1 : 0), state.queue.length), total: state.queue.length, ...(finished ? { status: 'done' as const } : {}) });
    try { localStorage.setItem(DEVICE_KEY, JSON.stringify(state)); } catch { /* the account copy still holds it */ }
  }, [state, record, touched, finished]);
  useEffect(() => { if (state.picked !== null) next.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true }); }, [state.picked]);
  useEffect(() => { if (finished) heading.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true }); }, [finished]);

  const progress = <div className="iw-progress" role="progressbar" aria-label="Cards done" aria-valuemin={0} aria-valuemax={state.queue.length} aria-valuenow={Math.min(state.index, state.queue.length)}><span style={{ width: `${Math.min(state.index, state.queue.length) / state.queue.length * 100}%` }}/></div>;

  if (finished) {
    const summary = summarize(state);
    return <section className="iw-deck" aria-label="Indicator word flashcards">
      {progress}
      <div className="iw-summary">
        <h2 ref={heading} tabIndex={-1}>{summary.right} right first time.</h2>
        <p>{summary.missed === 0 ? 'No card needed a second look.' : `${summary.missed} needed a second look.`}{summary.stillMissing.length > 0 && ` Still to practice: ${summary.stillMissing.join(', ')}.`}</p>
        {state.earlier.length > 0 && <p className="iw-earlier">Earlier rounds: {state.earlier.map((r, i) => `Round ${i + 1}, ${r.right} of ${r.total}`).join(' · ')}.</p>}
        <button type="button" className="iw-primary" onClick={() => setState(previous => newRound(previous))}><RotateCcw size={17} aria-hidden="true"/>Go again</button>
      </div>
    </section>;
  }

  const entry = state.queue[state.index];
  const card = cardById(entry.id)!;
  const answered = state.picked !== null;
  const right = answered && state.picked === card.group;
  const firstCount = indicatorWordCards.length;
  const position = entry.retry ? `Second look ${state.index - firstCount + 1} of ${state.queue.length - firstCount}` : `Card ${state.index + 1} of ${firstCount}`;
  const lastCard = state.index === state.queue.length - 1;
  const choose = (group: IndicatorGroup) => setState(previous => pick(previous, group));

  return <section className="iw-deck" aria-label="Indicator word flashcards">
    {progress}
    <p className="iw-count">{position}</p>
    <div className="iw-stage" data-result={answered ? (right ? 'right' : 'wrong') : undefined}>
      <div className="iw-card" data-flipped={answered}>
        <div className="iw-face iw-front" aria-hidden={answered}>
          <span className="iw-eyebrow">Which group?</span>
          <span className="iw-word">{displayWord(card.word)}</span>
        </div>
        <div className="iw-face iw-back" aria-hidden={!answered}>
          <span className="iw-verdict">{right ? 'Right' : 'Not quite'}</span>
          <span className="iw-line"><strong>{displayWord(card.word)}.</strong> {groupSentence(card.group)}</span>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
    <p className="iw-sr" role="status">{answered ? `${right ? 'Right' : 'Not quite'}. ${displayWord(card.word)}. ${groupSentence(card.group)}` : ''}</p>
    {answered
      ? <button ref={next} type="button" className="iw-primary" onClick={() => setState(advance)}>{lastCard ? 'See my results' : 'Next card'}<ArrowRight size={17} aria-hidden="true"/></button>
      : <div className="iw-choices" role="group" aria-label={`Which group is ${card.word}?`}>
        <button type="button" onClick={() => choose(1)}>Group 1</button>
        <button type="button" onClick={() => choose(2)}>Group 2</button>
      </div>}
  </section>;
}
import { indicatorWordCards, type IndicatorGroup, type IndicatorWordCard } from './content/indicatorWordCards';

export type Outcome = 'right' | 'wrong';
export interface QueueEntry { id: string; retry: boolean }
/** Everything the deck needs to resume exactly where the student stopped. */
export interface DeckState {
  v: 1;
  round: number;
  queue: QueueEntry[];
  index: number;
  /** The group the student chose on the current card, until they move on. */
  picked: IndicatorGroup | null;
  results: Record<string, { first?: Outcome; second?: Outcome }>;
  earlier: { right: number; total: number }[];
}

const byId = new Map(indicatorWordCards.map(card => [card.id, card]));
export const cardById = (id: string): IndicatorWordCard | undefined => byId.get(id);

export function mulberry32(seed: number) {
  return () => {
    seed = (seed + 0x6D2B79F5) | 0;
    let t = Math.imul(seed ^ (seed >>> 15), 1 | seed);
    t = (t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), 61 | t)) ^ t;
    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
  };
}

function shuffled<T>(list: T[], rng: () => number): T[] {
  const copy = [...list];
  for (let i = copy.length - 1; i > 0; i -= 1) {
    const j = Math.floor(rng() * (i + 1));
    [copy[i], copy[j]] = [copy[j], copy[i]];
  }
  return copy;
}
export function longestRun(cards: IndicatorWordCard[]): number {
  let best = 0; let run = 0;
  cards.forEach((card, i) => { run = i && cards[i - 1].group === card.group ? run + 1 : 1; best = Math.max(best, run); });
  return best;
}
const triples = (cards: IndicatorWordCard[]) => cards.reduce((n, card, i) => n + (i >= 2 && cards[i - 1].group === card.group && cards[i - 2].group === card.group ? 1 : 0), 0);
/** A true shuffle, then random swaps until no group repeats more than twice in a row. Some back-to-backs stay; long streaks do not. */
export function shuffleSpread(cards: IndicatorWordCard[], rng: () => number = Math.random): IndicatorWordCard[] {
  const order = shuffled(cards, rng);
  let bad = triples(order);
  for (let step = 0; step < 4000 && bad > 0; step += 1) {
    const i = Math.floor(rng() * order.length);
    const j = Math.floor(rng() * order.length);
    if (i === j || order[i].group === order[j].group) continue;
    [order[i], order[j]] = [order[j], order[i]];
    const now = triples(order);
    if (now <= bad) bad = now; else [order[i], order[j]] = [order[j], order[i]];
  }
  return order;
}

export function newRound(previous?: DeckState, rng: () => number = Math.random): DeckState {
  return {
    v: 1, round: previous ? previous.round + 1 : 1,
    queue: shuffleSpread(indicatorWordCards, rng).map(card => ({ id: card.id, retry: false })),
    index: 0, picked: null, results: {},
    earlier: previous ? [...previous.earlier, { right: summarize(previous).right, total: indicatorWordCards.length }] : [],
  };
}

export const isFinished = (state: DeckState) => state.index >= state.queue.length;

/** Record the student's choice. A first-pass miss queues that card once, at the very end of the round. */
export function pick(state: DeckState, choice: IndicatorGroup): DeckState {
  const entry = state.queue[state.index];
  const card = entry && cardById(entry.id);
  if (!card || state.picked !== null) return state;
  const outcome: Outcome = choice === card.group ? 'right' : 'wrong';
  const prior = state.results[card.id] ?? {};
  const results = { ...state.results, [card.id]: entry.retry ? { ...prior, second: outcome } : { ...prior, first: outcome } };
  const queue = outcome === 'wrong' && !entry.retry ? [...state.queue, { id: card.id, retry: true }] : state.queue;
  return { ...state, picked: choice, results, queue };
}
export function advance(state: DeckState): DeckState {
  return state.picked === null ? state : { ...state, index: state.index + 1, picked: null };
}

export function summarize(state: DeckState) {
  const firstTotal = state.queue.filter(entry => !entry.retry).length;
  const entries = Object.entries(state.results);
  const right = entries.filter(([, r]) => r.first === 'right').length;
  const missed = entries.filter(([, r]) => r.first === 'wrong').length;
  const stillMissing = entries.filter(([, r]) => r.first === 'wrong' && r.second === 'wrong').map(([id]) => cardById(id)!.word);
  return { right, missed, firstTotal, stillMissing };
}

/** Accepts only a state that still matches the deck; anything else starts a fresh round rather than resuming into a broken one. */
export function readDeckState(raw: unknown): DeckState | null {
  const value = raw as Partial<DeckState> | null;
  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object' || value.v !== 1 || !Number.isInteger(value.round) || !Number.isInteger(value.index)) return null;
  if (!Array.isArray(value.queue) || !value.queue.every(entry => entry && typeof entry.id === 'string' && byId.has(entry.id) && typeof entry.retry === 'boolean')) return null;
  const firstPass = value.queue.filter(entry => !entry.retry);
  if (firstPass.length !== indicatorWordCards.length || new Set(firstPass.map(entry => entry.id)).size !== indicatorWordCards.length) return null;
  if (value.index! < 0 || value.index! > value.queue.length) return null;
  if (value.picked !== null && value.picked !== 1 && value.picked !== 2) return null;
  if (value.picked !== null && value.index === value.queue.length) return null;
  if (!value.results || typeof value.results !== 'object') return null;
  const outcome = (v: unknown) => v === undefined || v === 'right' || v === 'wrong';
  if (!Object.entries(value.results).every(([id, r]) => byId.has(id) && !!r && outcome(r.first) && outcome(r.second))) return null;
  const earlier = Array.isArray(value.earlier) ? value.earlier.filter(r => r && Number.isInteger(r.right) && Number.isInteger(r.total)) : [];
  return { v: 1, round: value.round!, queue: value.queue, index: value.index!, picked: value.picked ?? null, results: value.results, earlier };
}
/** The indicator-word deck. One card per word, both groups mixed. Word list confirmed by Joshua 2026-10-11. */
export type IndicatorGroup = 1 | 2;
export interface IndicatorWordCard { id: string; word: string; group: IndicatorGroup }

const group1 = ['if', 'when', 'whenever', 'where', 'all', 'every', 'any', 'anyone', 'anytime', 'every time', 'each', 'the only', 'who'];
const group2 = ['only', 'only if', 'only when', 'only where', 'always', 'must', 'requires'];
const slug = (word: string) => word.replace(/\s+/g, '-');

export const indicatorWordCards: IndicatorWordCard[] = [
  ...group1.map(word => ({ id: `g1-${slug(word)}`, word, group: 1 as const })),
  ...group2.map(word => ({ id: `g2-${slug(word)}`, word, group: 2 as const })),
];

export const displayWord = (word: string) => word.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + word.slice(1);
export const groupKind = (group: IndicatorGroup) => group === 1 ? 'sufficient' : 'necessary';
/** The one line on the back of every card. */
export const groupSentence = (group: IndicatorGroup) => `This is a ${groupKind(group)} condition indicator word, Group ${group}.`;

.iw-deck{max-width:560px;margin:28px auto;display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:18px;color:#173544}
.iw-progress{height:4px;background:#e7ece4;border-radius:2px;overflow:hidden}
.iw-progress>span{display:block;height:100%;background:#bc7948;transition:width .35s ease}
.iw-count{margin:0;font-size:13px;letter-spacing:.12em;text-transform:uppercase;font-weight:700}
.iw-stage{perspective:1100px}
.iw-card{position:relative;min-height:230px;transform-style:preserve-3d;transition:transform .5s cubic-bezier(.3,.7,.3,1)}
.iw-card[data-flipped=true]{transform:rotateY(180deg)}
.iw-stage[data-result=wrong] .iw-card{transition-delay:.4s}
.iw-stage[data-result=wrong]{animation:iw-shake .4s ease-in-out}
.iw-face{position:absolute;inset:0;display:flex;flex-direction:column;align-items:center;justify-content:center;gap:14px;padding:28px;text-align:center;border:1px solid #cfd9d3;border-radius:16px;background:#fffdf7;backface-visibility:hidden;-webkit-backface-visibility:hidden;box-shadow:0 10px 26px #18354410}
.iw-back{transform:rotateY(180deg)}
.iw-stage[data-result=right] .iw-back{border-color:#2f7d5d;background:#eaf5ee}
.iw-stage[data-result=wrong] .iw-back{border-color:#b4472f;background:#fbece7}
.iw-eyebrow,.iw-verdict{font-size:12px;letter-spacing:.14em;text-transform:uppercase;font-weight:700;color:#5b6f78}
.iw-stage[data-result=right] .iw-verdict{color:#1f6a4a}
.iw-stage[data-result=wrong] .iw-verdict{color:#a23b25}
.iw-word{font:clamp(38px,8vw,56px)/1.1 Georgia,serif;color:#173544}
.iw-line{font-size:19px;line-height:1.5;color:#173544;max-width:34ch}
.iw-choices{display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr;gap:12px}
.iw-choices button{padding:18px 12px;font-size:19px;font-weight:700;color:#173544;background:#fffdf7;border:2px solid #254d65;border-radius:12px;cursor:pointer;transition:background .15s,transform .1s}
.iw-choices button:hover{background:#e6eef1}
.iw-choices button:active{transform:scale(.98)}
.iw-primary{display:inline-flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;gap:12px;align-self:flex-start;border:0;border-radius:8px;background:#254d65;color:#fff;padding:15px 22px;font-size:16px;font-weight:700;cursor:pointer}
.iw-summary{padding:8px 0}
.iw-summary h2{font:32px/1.2 Georgia,serif;margin:0 0 10px;outline:none}
.iw-summary p{font-size:18px;line-height:1.6;margin:0 0 14px}
.iw-earlier{font-size:15px!important;color:#5b6f78}
.iw-sr{position:absolute;width:1px;height:1px;overflow:hidden;clip:rect(0 0 0 0);margin:-1px;padding:0}
.iw-deck :focus-visible{outline:3px solid #b86632;outline-offset:3px}

it('keeps the word deck on the same answered card across page navigation', () => {
  localStorage.clear();
  render(lesson('group-2'));
  jumpTo('Recall the indicators');
  expect(screen.getByText('Card 1 of 20')).toBeVisible();
  const word = document.querySelector('.iw-front .iw-word')!.textContent;
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next card' })).toBeVisible();
  turn('Next');
  turn('Back');
  expect(screen.getByText('Card 1 of 20')).toBeVisible();
  expect(document.querySelector('.iw-front .iw-word')!.textContent).toBe(word);
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next card' })).toBeVisible();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next card' }));
  expect(screen.getByText('Card 2 of 20')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Group 2' })).toBeEnabled();
});

it('requires an explicit end-page action to record reading, including after a contents jump', async () => {
  const complete = vi.fn();
  render(lesson('group-1', complete));
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Mark lesson as read' })).toBeNull();
  turn('Next');
  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Mark lesson as read' })).toBeNull();
  jumpTo('Keep the direction and the whole condition');
  await waitFor(() => expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Keep the direction and the whole condition' })).toHaveFocus());
  expect(screen.getByRole('navigation', { name: 'Lesson pages' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Mark lesson as read' }));
  expect(complete).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
  turn('Back');
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Mark lesson as read' })).toBeNull();
  expect(complete).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
});

it('starts a changed unit at its first page and only hosts the mixed deck once both groups are taught', () => {
  localStorage.clear();
  const { rerender } = render(lesson('group-1'));
  jumpTo('Recall the indicators');
  expect(screen.getByText(/flashcards mix Group 1 and Group 2 words/)).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeNull();

  rerender(lesson('group-2'));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Find what is required' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeNull();
  jumpTo('Recall the indicators');
  expect(screen.getByText('Card 1 of 20')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeVisible();

  rerender(lesson('group-1'));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Find the sufficient idea' })).toBeVisible();
  jumpTo('Recall the indicators');
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeNull();
});

it('separates a present negative requirement from its absence in the fresh two-room exercise', () => {
  render(lesson('orientation'));
  jumpTo('Two possible rooms, one limit');
import { cleanup, fireEvent, render, screen, within } from '@testing-library/react';
import { MemoryRouter, useLocation } from 'react-router-dom';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { OpeningCourseScreen } from './OpeningCourse';
import { flashcardUrl } from './FlashcardTools';
beforeEach(() => localStorage.clear());
afterEach(cleanup);
const url = (id: string) => `/dev/conditional-opening-preview?page=${id}`;
function Harness() {
  const location = useLocation();
  return <OpeningCourseScreen view={new URLSearchParams(location.search).get('page') || 'lessons'} url={url}/>;
}
it('opens the mixed word deck from the Group 1 list, and returns to the lesson', () => {
  render(<MemoryRouter initialEntries={[url('sufficient')]}><Harness/></MemoryRouter>);
  const list = screen.getByRole('region', { name: 'Group 1 indicator words' });
  expect(within(list).getByRole('link', { name: 'Go to flashcards' })).toHaveAttribute('href', flashcardUrl(url, 'indicators', undefined, 'sufficient'));
  fireEvent.click(within(list).getByRole('link', { name: 'Go to flashcards' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'Indicator words' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.getByText('Which group?')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Group 2' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('combobox')).toBeNull();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('link', { name: 'Back to sufficient condition' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'The Sufficient Condition' })).toBeVisible();
});
it('lists one word deck beside the concept cards, and sends old Group 1 and Group 2 links to it', () => {
  render(<MemoryRouter initialEntries={[url('necessary')]}><Harness/></MemoryRouter>);
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('link', { name: 'Go to flashcards' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'Indicator words' })).toBeVisible();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('link', { name: 'All flashcard sets' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Terms and concepts' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Indicator words' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('heading', { name: /Group 1: Conditional indicator words/ })).toBeNull();
  expect(screen.getAllByRole('link', { name: 'Go to flashcards' })).toHaveLength(2);
  cleanup();
  render(<MemoryRouter initialEntries={[`${url('tools')}&deck=group-2`]}><Harness/></MemoryRouter>);
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'Indicator words' })).toBeVisible();
});
it('does not expose a disallowed set through the query', () => {
  render(<MemoryRouter initialEntries={[`${url('tools')}&deck=group-2`]}><OpeningCourseScreen view="tools" steps={[]} url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  expect(screen.getByText('No flashcards are available in this work.')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeNull();
});

it('exposes only the permitted content and flashcard families', () => {
  const steps = OPENING_COURSE_PATH.filter(step => step.unitId === 'group-1');
  const { rerender } = render(<MemoryRouter><OpeningCourseScreen view="necessary" steps={steps} url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'This lesson is not available here.' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(document.querySelector('.opening-course-content')).toBeNull();
  rerender(<MemoryRouter><OpeningCourseScreen view="tools" steps={steps} url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  // The word deck mixes both groups, so Group 1 work alone opens no deck.
  expect(screen.getByText('No flashcards are available in this work.')).toBeVisible();
  const both = OPENING_COURSE_PATH.filter(step => step.unitId === 'group-1' || step.unitId === 'group-2');
  rerender(<MemoryRouter><OpeningCourseScreen view="tools" steps={both} url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Indicator words' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('heading', { name: 'Terms and concepts' })).toBeNull();
});


function CourseNavigationHarness() {
  const location = useLocation();
  const view = location.pathname.split('/').pop() || 'lessons';
  return <><OpeningCourseNavigation view={view} url={url}/><OpeningCourseScreen view={view} url={url}/></>;
}

it('teaches every Group 1 indicator before the sufficient-side drill, then the complete phrase', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="sufficient-words"/>);
  const tabs = within(screen.getByRole('group', { name: 'Choose an indicator' })).getAllByRole('button').map(tab => tab.textContent?.replace(/[^A-Za-z ]/g, '').replace(/ opened$/, '').trim());
  expect(tabs).toEqual(['If', 'When', 'Where', 'All', 'The only', 'Every', 'Any']);
  expect(screen.getByText(/Work through all seven indicators/)).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Recall the indicators' })).toBeVisible();
  const text = document.body.textContent ?? '';
  // Words the drills test: when, whenever, where, each, every, any, people who.
  for (const word of [/\bWhenever\b/, /\bEach\b/, /people who/i, /\bWhere\b/]) expect(text).toMatch(word);
  // The complete-condition note no longer repeats the report and late-applicant examples the indicator note teaches.
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Outside Drawer R' })).toHaveAttribute('aria-pressed', 'true');
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Blue border' })).toHaveAttribute('aria-pressed', 'true');
  expect(screen.getByText('This fits the rule and disproves the reverse.')).toBeVisible();
});

it('keeps the quiz deck out of the lessons: the recall page only offers a flashcards link', () => {
  localStorage.clear();
  render(lesson('group-2'));
  jumpTo('Recall the indicators');
  expect(screen.getByText('Practice these words on cards, one at a time.')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByText('Card 1 of 20')).toBeNull();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeNull();
});
});

it('starts a changed unit at its first page and keeps the flashcards out of the lesson', () => {
  localStorage.clear();
  const { rerender } = render(lesson('group-1'));
  jumpTo('Recall the indicators');
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeNull();

  rerender(lesson('group-2'));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Find what is required' })).toBeVisible();
  jumpTo('Recall the indicators');
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Group 1' })).toBeNull();

  rerender(lesson('group-1'));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Find the sufficient idea' })).toBeVisible();
});

# Grill Me

One skill, self-contained. It holds both the **interview method** (Step 2) and the **capture discipline** (Steps 1 and 3): every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up. It does not call the vendor `grilling` skill: the method is written inline below so this skill can add the capture steps around it and keep working even if `grilling` changes upstream.

If the topic is a feature, component, or other implementation work rather than an open-ended plan or decision, `brainstorming` runs a similar interview but gates on a written, approved design doc before any code — reach for that instead when the destination is implementation.

## Step 1: set up the capture file (BEFORE the first question)

1. Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`; in an established project follow its declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.
2. Create the capture file at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create `brainstorms/` if missing). Every capture lives here, one predictable home regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. A polished deliverable (plan, map, spec) may later move into the relevant `projects/` folder; the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
   - Get today's real date from the clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
   - Never overwrite an existing capture. Same date and topic: add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user asks or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.
   - No topic supplied or inferable: create an `untitled-discovery` capture with goal pending, ask what to explore, record the chosen topic and goal in that same file.
3. Write the header immediately: title, date, goal of the session, empty "Open flags" section.
4. Tell the user where you are saving, in one line.

## Step 2: run the interview

Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.

Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you have not heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.

Format a round like so:

```
**Q1 - <question title>**: <question body, may be multiple paragraphs, including choices>

Recommended: <your recommended answer>

---

**Q2 - <question title>**: ...

Recommended: ...
```

Each round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.

Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, live site), look it up or dispatch a sub-agent; never ask the user for anything you could find yourself. Do not block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.

The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch visited, nothing silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

Two adjustments the capture layer imposes on the method:

- For **decisions**, give a recommended answer grounded in context and label it as a recommendation. For **personal or business facts**, ask neutrally. Never lead with invented facts, and never save your inference as the user's answer.
- When the user **cannot answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Do not stall the round on it.

## Step 3: the checkpoint rule (non-negotiable)

After EVERY round of answers, BEFORE you ask the next round:

- Append one structured entry per answered question: the question, the key facts and decisions from the answer (in the user's words where wording matters), and any flags (things they could not answer, plus who should).
- Update the running summary when a later answer changes it. Preserve the original entry, mark it superseded, and link to the correcting answer so the reasoning stays traceable.
- Read back the saved entries before continuing. If the write fails, say so, keep the answers visible in chat, and do not claim they were saved or collect more answers without a working checkpoint.
- Only then ask the next round.

Never let two rounds pile up in your head before writing. The file, not your context, is the source of truth. Never make the user ask you to save progress.

Keep user-confirmed facts, tentative ideas, your recommendations, and unresolved questions distinguishable in the capture.

## Capture file structure

```
# {Topic}: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes
Date: {date} · Goal: {one line}
Status: in progress / paused / complete
Context sources: {relevant existing pages, if any}

## Summary / key decisions
(running synthesis, updated as you go)

## Q&A log
### Round 1
#### Q1 - {topic}
- Asked: {question}
- Recommended: {your recommendation, labeled}
- Captured: {user-confirmed facts and decisions, in their words where it matters}
- Tentative: {unconfirmed ideas, clearly labeled}
- Flags: {open item -> owner}
#### Q2 - ...
### Round 2
...

## Open flags (pending input)
- {item} -> {who can answer}
```

## At the end or on pause

Respect an explicit stop immediately; do not add a new question. Near the end, offer a completeness backstop ("anything we haven't touched?"). Then:

- Read the capture for contradictions or gaps. Mark unresolved conflicts explicitly; do not choose a business fact on the user's behalf. Update status and resume point.
- For sessions explicitly about building or updating AI OS context, merge user-confirmed durable facts and preferences into the appropriate existing context page, with a dated link back to the capture. Preserve unrelated content. Record confirmed meaningful decisions in the existing decision log without duplicating entries. Unknowns, brainstorm ideas, and your recommendations stay labeled in the capture until confirmed.
- For a plan or design interview without a request to update canonical context, the raw capture is the output. Suggest `/link` for routing a useful result, or `/level-up` for turning a selected opportunity into one improvement. Do not run either automatically.
- Ensure the operating manual or an existing index routes to `brainstorms/` when capture routing is absent, using the project's conventions and preserving shared manual parity. Individual captures do not each need a root-manual entry. Raw captures are dated interview evidence, not automatically current business truth.
- Give a short recap: clickable capture path, context pages actually updated, remaining flags, next step or resume point.

Do not act on the plan until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

## Boundaries and verification

- Scope is the local interview capture and any requested context updates. No global-memory edits, publication, messages, or external-system changes. Do not request or save credentials; use references or redactions for secrets.
- The starter kit ignores `brainstorms/` in Git by default. This does not remove captures already tracked in an existing repository.
- Validate a fresh session, a same-day name collision, a resume, a correcting answer, a stop request, and a failed write. Verify that only confirmed facts reach canonical context and that earlier answers remain traceable.

# Conditional indicator flashcards: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes
Date: 2026-10-11 · Goal: Pin down the redesigned indicator flashcards (indicator word on the front, student commits to a group, card flips, back says the group and what to do with it)
Status: built and pushed to PR #204 (2026-10-11). Resume point: none; open items are the Group 1 lesson decision (below) and the preview of how it reads in the course.
Context sources: `ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/conditionals/` (`components/StudyDeck.tsx`, `components/FlashcardTools.tsx`, `content/indicatorFlashcards.ts`)

## What exists today (verified 2026-10-11, from the code)
- 30 cards: 4 "foundation" (concepts), 16 Group 1, 10 Group 2. Each front is a full question ("Every folder marked complete contains a signed form. Which condition is enough...") and the back is the explanation. Not the indicator word alone.
- Student picks an indicator from a dropdown inside a focused deck, taps the card to flip it. Nothing is committed before the flip.
- Decks are reached from Tools > Flashcards (3 sets) and embedded as "Recall" steps in the lessons.

## Joshua's request, as first stated (verbatim intent)
- Front: just the indicator word or phrase ("only", "only if", "always", "if").
- The student must say which group it falls under. The card flips after they say it.
- Back: which group it is, and what to do with that concept.
- Mentions "five cards" for "the individual indicator" cards. Meaning not yet pinned down.

## Summary / key decisions
Confirmed by Joshua (round 1):
- The 'five cards' remark was a slip. He meant flashcards. No five-card rule.
- The new indicator-word cards REPLACE the Group 1 and Group 2 decks.
- Two buttons under the card, Group 1 or Group 2. No typing.
- Choosing a button flips the card and says right or wrong.
- A wrong pick still flips. The screen shakes, the card flips, and it says what the right group is and what was wrong.
- Back text: very short, one or two lines, mainly which group it is. Clear, organized, not complicated. Quick feedback.

## Round 2 decisions (confirmed by Joshua, 2026-10-11)
- Mixed groups only. One deck, both groups together.
- Include ALL indicator words from both groups (not my 21-word cut). Do not label the groups on the deck listing; the cards are mixed.
- Group wording: Group 2 is a "necessary condition indicator"; by the same pattern Group 1 is a "sufficient condition indicator" (my inference for Group 1).
- Back of the card: "This is a necessary condition indicator word, Group 2." One line, group named, in those words.
- Order truly randomized. No long same-group runs; a few back-to-backs are fine.

import { createContext, useContext } from 'react';

export const CourseFlashcardUrl = createContext<OpeningCourseUrl | null>(null);
const labels: Record<string, string> = {
  'g1-if-first': 'if', 'g1-if-last': 'if: condition comes last', 'g1-when-first': 'when', 'g1-when-last': 'when: condition comes last',
  'g1-where': 'where', 'g1-all': 'all', 'g1-every': 'every', 'g1-any': 'any', 'g1-the-only': 'the only', 'g1-target-later': 'every: condition comes last',
  'g1-whenever': 'whenever', 'g1-anytime': 'anytime', 'g1-every-time': 'every time', 'g1-in-order-to': 'in order to', 'g1-people-who': 'people who', 'g1-each': 'each',
  'g2-only': 'only', 'g2-only-if': 'only if', 'g2-if-contrast': 'if / only if', 'g2-only-when': 'only when', 'g2-only-where': 'only where',
  'g2-always': 'always', 'g2-requires': 'requires', 'g2-required-before': 'required for', 'g2-must-instruction': 'must', 'g2-only-the-only': 'only / the only', 'g2-necessary-before': 'necessary for',
  'foundation-enough': 'Sufficient condition', 'foundation-required': 'Necessary condition', 'foundation-reverse': 'Reversing the arrow', 'foundation-rule-not-fact': 'Rules and facts',
};
export function flashcardUrl(url: OpeningCourseUrl, group: string, card?: string, from?: string) {
  const target = url('tools');
  const [path, query = ''] = target.split('?');
  const params = new URLSearchParams(query);
  params.set('deck', group);
  if (card) params.set('card', card);
  if (from) params.set('from', from);
  return `${path}?${params}`;
}

];

    <header data-course-heading><p className="oc-label">Flashcards</p><h1>{set.title}</h1><p>{set.description}</p></header>
    <IndicatorWordDeck/>

import { useEffect, useId, useState } from 'react';
import { indicatorFlashcards } from '../content/indicatorFlashcards';

export function StudyDeck({ group, selectedCard, onCardChange, choices }: { group: string; selectedCard?: string; onCardChange?: (id: string) => void; choices?: { id: string; label: string }[] }) {
  const cards = indicatorFlashcards.filter(card => card.group === group).sort((a, b) => choices ? choices.findIndex(item => item.id === a.id) - choices.findIndex(item => item.id === b.id) : 0);
  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);
  const [flipped, setFlipped] = useState(false);
  const [revisit, setRevisit] = useState<string[]>([]);
  const label = useId();
  useEffect(() => setFlipped(false), [selectedCard]);
  const focused = !!choices;
  const activeIndex = focused ? Math.max(0, cards.findIndex(item => item.id === selectedCard)) : index;
  const card = cards[activeIndex];
  if (!card) return null;
  const move = (next: number) => { setIndex(next); onCardChange?.(cards[next].id); setFlipped(false); };
  return <div className={`co-study-deck${focused ? " co-focused-deck" : ""}`}>
    {choices && <label className="co-expression-picker">{group === 'foundation' ? 'Choose a concept' : 'Choose an indicator'}<select value={card.id} onChange={event => move(cards.findIndex(item => ite
    <div className="co-deck-meta"><span id={label}>{focused ? "Example" : "Recall card"} {activeIndex + 1} of {cards.length}</span><span>{focused ? "" : "Think before you turn"}</span></div>
    <button type="button" className="co-study-card" data-flipped={flipped} aria-describedby={label} aria-expanded={flipped} onClick={() => setFlipped(!flipped)}>
      <span className="co-card-eyebrow">{focused ? (flipped ? 'Explanation' : 'Your question') : (flipped ? 'Compare your reasoning' : 'Explain it in your own words')}</span>
      <span className="co-card-text" key={`${card.id}-${flipped}`}>{flipped ? card.back : card.front}</span>
      <span className="co-card-turn">{focused ? (flipped ? 'Show question' : 'Show explanation') : (flipped ? 'Turn back to the question' : 'Turn to the explanation')} <span aria-hidden="true">↻</sp
    </button>
    <div className="co-deck-controls">
      <button type="button" disabled={activeIndex === 0} onClick={() => move(activeIndex - 1)}>{focused ? "Previous example" : "Previous card"}</button>
      {!focused && <button type="button" aria-pressed={revisit.includes(card.id)} onClick={() => setRevisit(items => items.includes(card.id) ? items.filter(id => id !== card.id) : [...items, card.id])
      <button type="button" disabled={activeIndex === cards.length - 1} onClick={() => move(activeIndex + 1)}>{focused ? "Next example" : "Next card"}</button>
    </div>
    {revisit.length > 0 && <div className="co-revisit"><p>{revisit.length} {revisit.length === 1 ? 'card' : 'cards'} to revisit. Try again after another page.</p><button type="button" onClick={() => m
  </div>;
}

## assistant

Yes, there is already a contrapositive lesson, and there are drills after it. I have not judged how strong the lesson is.

**What exists now (from `openingCoursePath.ts`)**
- **"The contrapositive"** (`missing-requirement`) is an early note. It uses the law-school and student example and says "if the necessary condition is false, the sufficient condition must be false too." It also separates the contrapositive from simply reversing the arrow.
- **"Write and read the contrapositive"** (`contrapositive`) is the main note. It says to reverse the two sides and negate both complete conditions. It includes the five-step routine and the "should I always write it?" question.
- **Four drills** follow it:
  - Revisit Drill 4: Add the contrapositive.
  - Revisit Drill 5: Negate the whole condition.
  - Check your contrapositive.
  - Drill 6: Build both diagrams.

I can audit those two notes and four drills against how you want contrapositives taught. I'd rate the lesson and report gaps, without rewriting it. Do you want that?

**Flashcard ideas for the key-terms deck**

| Front | Back |
|---|---|
| What is the contrapositive of "If A, then B"? | If not B, then not A. |
| What are the two steps to write a contrapositive? | Reverse the sides. Negate both. |
| If B is false in "If A, then B", what do you know about A? | A is false too. |
| Is "If not A, then not B" the contrapositive? | No. It negates without reversing, and the rule does not guarantee it. |
| What is the contrapositive of "If A and B, then C"? | If not C, then not A **or** not B. |
| Does the contrapositive say something new? | No. It is the same rule read from the other side. |
| What does "A only if B" mean? | If A, then B. |

**My recommendation:** add the first six, rename the deck to "Rules and contrapositives", and leave out "only if". It is already in the indicator deck. I'd also want you to check the "and" card, because "not A or not B" is the most error-prone one for students. I'd write the wording again and you would review it.

Should I add those cards, run the lesson audit, or both?

function MissingRequirementNote() {
  const [attempted, setAttempted] = useState(false);
  return <>
    <NoteSection title="What if someone is not a student?">
      <p>Keep the rule from the last lesson:</p>
      <blockquote>If you are enrolled in law school, then you are a student.</blockquote>
      <p>Now you learn that Maya is <strong>not a student</strong>. Think about what enrollment would require before choosing.</p>
      <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Could Maya be enrolled in law school while this rule holds?" options={['Yes, she could still be enrolled.', 'No, she cannot be enrolled.', 'The rule leaves her enrollment unknown.']} correct={1} onAttempt={choice => setAttempted(choice !== null)} feedback={['Enrollment would require Maya to be a student. That conflicts with the fact that she is not a student.', 'If Maya were enrolled, she would have to be a student. She is not a student, so she cannot be enrolled under this rule.', 'This fact settles enrollment. An enrolled person who is not a student would break the rule.']}/>
      {attempted && <div className="cr-discovery-reasoning">
        <h3>The missing requirement rules out enrollment</h3>
        <p>That is the <strong>contrapositive</strong>: <strong>if the necessary condition is false, the sufficient condition must be false too.</strong></p>
        <p>Being a student is required for enrollment. When that requirement is missing, enrollment is ruled out. We have not added a new rule; this follows from the rule we already had.</p>
        <figure className="cr-contrapositive-pair">
          <figcaption>Two ways to read the same rule</figcaption>
          <p><strong>Original</strong></p><RuleDiagram left="Enrolled in law school" right="Is a student"/>
          <p><strong>Contrapositive</strong></p><RuleDiagram left="Is not a student" right="Not enrolled in law school"/>
        </figure>
        <p>This is different from simply reversing the arrow. <strong>Being a student does not prove enrollment.</strong> A student could be studying history instead. The contrapositive starts with <strong>not being a student</strong>.</p>
      </div>}
    </NoteSection>
    {attempted && <ReferenceBody number={7} omitTransition/>}
  </>;
}

function ContrapositiveDiscovery() {
  const [attempted, setAttempted] = useState(false);
  return <div className="cr-discovery"><p>Use this stated rule for a different case.</p><blockquote>If a report is in the published archive, it has an approval stamp.</blockquote>
    <p>A report in that archive has to carry the stamp. Now you inspect Report K. It has no approval stamp.</p>
    <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Could Report K be in the published archive while this rule holds?" options={['Yes, its location is unrestricted.', 'No, it cannot be in that archive.', 'Yes, the missing stamp puts it in the archive.']} correct={1} onAttempt={choice => setAttempted(choice !== null)} feedback={['Its location is restricted by the rule. If K were in the archive, it would have a stamp, which conflicts with the given fact.', 'An archived K would have to have a stamp. K has no stamp, so it cannot be in the published archive under this rule.', 'The missing stamp rules out archive membership. It does not prove it.']}/>
    {attempted && <div className="cr-discovery-reasoning"><p>That way of using the rule is the <strong>contrapositive</strong>: when the necessary condition is absent, the sufficient condition cannot be present.</p>
      <p>It is the same rule. We didn't add a second rule; we worked out a consequence already contained in the first one.</p>
      <blockquote>{raffleRule}</blockquote>
      <p>Here, “you don't get a bicycle” means you never receive the promised outcome. It does not mean you're still waiting for a prize that will arrive later.</p>
      <ReferenceQuestion prompt="You don't get a bicycle. What follows about winning the raffle?" options={["You don't win the raffle.", 'You win the raffle.', 'Nothing follows about winning.']} correct={0} feedback={['Winning would require getting a bicycle. Since that outcome does not occur, you cannot have won under the rule.', 'Winning without getting a bicycle would break the rule. The absent requirement excludes winning.', 'The missing requirement does give an inference. If you won, you would get a bicycle.']}/>
      <p>Don't confuse that with starting from a requirement that is present. You could get a bicycle as a gift without winning. Let's compare all four starting facts.</p>
    </div>}
  </div>;
}

export function RuleDiagram({ left, right, label }: { left: string; right: string; label?: string }) {
  return <div className="cr-diagram" role="img" aria-label={label || `${left} implies ${right}`}><strong>{left}</strong><span aria-hidden="true">→</span><strong>{right}</strong></div>;
}

      <details className="cr-extension"><summary>More Group 2 forms, with the rule intact</summary><p><strong>Only when</strong> and <strong>only where</strong> can restrict the occasions or places of an outcome: “The fountain runs only when its pump is on.” A running fountain requires an operating pump. The pump being on does not prove that the fountain runs.</p><p><strong>Requires</strong> directly names a requirement: “Entry requires a current pass.” Keep current. <strong>Always</strong> needs the rule's subject and scope: “The upstairs room is always quiet when the door is shut.” Here a shut door proves quiet, not the other way around.</p><p><strong>Only</strong> and <strong>the only</strong> are different complete indicators. “Only people who get a bicycle win this raffle” and “The only people who win this raffle are people who get a bicycle” both keep the rule: winning proves getting a bicycle.</p></details>
      <p>We can now recognize both roles before writing any symbols. Next, let's see what happens when a requirement is missing.</p>

    <ReferenceStep number={7} label="The four situations" title="Keep what follows separate from what stays open">
      <p>Keep the raffle rule. Choose a fact, then decide what it lets you conclude.</p>
      <FourCaseExplorer/>

      <p>Watch how the same law-school rule handles different facts about Maya.</p>
      <div className="cr-sufficiency"><SufficientConditionFilm/></div>
      <p>Starting from getting a bicycle and concluding that you win is the tempting <strong>reverse</strong>. Starting from not winning and concluding that you do not get a bicycle is the tempting <strong>inverse</strong>. Neither follows from this rule alone.</p>
      <p>The contrapositive starts from <strong>not getting a bicycle</strong> and proves <strong>not winning the raffle</strong>. Its reasoning depends on the missing required result, not on reversing whichever words happen to appear.</p>
      <p>We now understand the rule in words. Symbols will let us record that understanding without carrying every word of the rule.</p>
    </ReferenceStep>

=====
      <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Which diagram keeps the moved if-clause?" options={['Get bicycle → Win', 'Win → Get bicycle']} correct={1} feedback={['You let sentence order choose the arrow. The if-clause still makes winning enough for getting a bicycle.', 'The sufficient condition is still winning. Where it appears in the rule does not change what it proves.']}/>
    </ReferenceStep>

    <ReferenceStep number={9} label="Write and read the contrapositive" title="Write the meaning you already understand">
      <p>Win means you win the raffle. Get bicycle means you get a bicycle. Our original is Win → Get bicycle.</p>
      <p>We already know that not getting a bicycle rules out winning. Write that as <strong>~Get bicycle → ~Win</strong>. The <strong>~</strong> sign means <strong>not</strong>. Not getting the outcome is different from not having received it yet.</p>
      <p>To write a contrapositive from a diagram, reverse the two sides and negate <strong>both complete conditions</strong>. A condition already containing not loses that not when we negate it. Let's try all four patterns.</p>
      <SignPatterns/>
      <div className="cr-method"><p className="co-kicker">The complete five-step routine</p><ol><li><strong>Find the indicator.</strong> Read its job in the whole rule.</li><li><strong>Identify both complete ideas.</strong> Keep restrictions such as current or submitted by Friday.</li><li><strong>Place the roles.</strong> Sufficient goes left; necessary goes right. Choose labels you can read back.</li><li><strong>Write the contrapositive.</strong> Reverse the sides and negate both complete conditions.</li><li><strong>Read both in English.</strong> Compare them with what the original sentence actually requires.</li></ol></div>
      <p>The original and its contrapositive describe the same rule. If writing both helps you see that rule, write both. If you can read the contrapositive from the original when it is relevant, you needn't copy it merely to have a second line.</p>
      <p>Read an arrow forward to use its stated direction. To read its contrapositive, start from the right condition's absence and work back to the left condition's absence. That is a different move from reading the arrow backward unchanged.</p>
    </ReferenceStep>

    <ReferenceStep number={10} label="Practical diagramming questions" title="Use writing to make your reasoning easier to check">
      <div className="cr-faq">
        <details><summary>When should I diagram?</summary><p>While learning, write enough to make your choices visible. A difficult sentence, several rules or a connection you keep rereading can be a reason to put the rule on paper. With practice, you may hold a simple rule mentally. Use the writing to help the reasoning.</p></details>
        <details><summary>How do I check whether my diagram is right?</summary><p>Read it back and compare the whole meaning. Did you keep each restriction? Does your arrow promise something the original leaves open? When a diagram is wrong, record the exact phrase and the mistake it produced. That gives you something specific to practice. Familiarity alone is not proof of correctness.</p></details>
        <details><summary>What if writing takes time?</summary><p>Start by producing a correct, readable diagram. Speed is a later practice goal. Writing can reduce rereading, but it does not save time in every case. Compare how it helps on the rules you are actually working through. First make the rule accurate. Work on speed once you can explain why the arrow follows. </p></details>
        <details><summary>Should I always write the contrapositive?</summary><p>You need to understand it every time. Write it when it helps you use the missing requirement, inspect your translation or connect rules. Otherwise, you may read that direction from the original. Extra notation should have a job.</p></details>
        <aside className="cr-training-note"><h3>Why this practice asks for both</h3><p>In the integrated drill, write or choose the original and the contrapositive. We're checking that you can produce both. Once that skill is reliable, a performance task need not require both lines for every rule.</p></aside>
        <details><summary>How should I abbreviate the conditions?</summary><p>Use short labels that bring back the complete idea. W might mean win this raffle. Current pass may be clearer than a single letter if you're tracking several kinds of pass. Shortening should save writing without losing meaning. There is no fixed letter limit.</p></details>
      </div>

What needs repairing?" options={['The labels need to be longer.', 'The arrow makes the requirement enough.', 'Nothing; the first phrase always goes left.']} correct={1} feedback={['Longer labels would not repair the direction. The tray requires a paid stamp, but a stamped envelope can remain outside it.', 'Only restricts tray envelopes to ones with paid stamps. In tray → Paid stamp keeps that requirement; the proposed reverse promises tray placement to every stamped envelope.', 'Sentence order does not determine diagram order. Only identifies the paid stamp as required for tray placement.']}/>
      <p>That error came from the language. Let's inspect precisely which idea an indicator refers to.</p>
    </ReferenceStep>

## assistant

I read the notes and the sign-pattern practice in the code. I did not read the four drills in detail, view the pages in the browser, or test any of it with students. This is my judgment, not a measurement. Overall it is a solid lesson on the reasoning, but it does not yet give the clear, strong statement of what a contrapositive is that you want.

**What is strong**
- **Reasoning first.** Students work out the idea before they get the word. In the Maya case, they decide whether she could be enrolled if she is not a student. The Report K and raffle cases work the same way.
- **Three look-alikes kept apart.** It separates the contrapositive from the reverse and the inverse. The four-situations table and explorer show what follows and what stays open.
- **The four sign patterns are well built.** The cases are neither side negative, necessary side negative, sufficient side negative, and both negative. Each has a multiple-choice check with feedback on each wrong choice. A rule that already has a "not" is the case students usually miss.
- **Negating a whole condition has its own section.** "Signed and dated" is false if either part is missing, and "every" turns into "at least one is not". Number conditions are covered too.
- **Practice follows the lesson.** Four drills come after it, so students write and choose contrapositives.

**What is weak**
1. **No crisp statement.** The idea appears in two forms, in two places. The early note says "if the necessary condition is false, the sufficient condition must be false." The later note says "reverse the sides and negate both complete conditions." That second one is in a middle paragraph of step 9 and is never boxed. The five-step routine on that page is about translating a sentence, and only step 4 is the contrapositive.
2. **It undercuts itself.** It tells students they "needn't copy it" and asks "Should I always write the contrapositive?" You said it is very important, and this reads as optional.
3. **Compound conditions are not tied to the contrapositive.** I did not find a worked example of "If A and B, then C" becoming "If not C, then not A or not B". The negation section has "signed and dated" but not inside a contrapositive. This is the most error-prone case.
4. **No LSAT payoff.** I found no real argument-style question that the contrapositive solves, so students don't see why it matters.
5. **Dilution.** Five unrelated "when should I diagram?" questions sit inside the contrapositive note.
6. **Spacing and clunky lines.** The first contrapositive note is 5th on the path and the formal one is about 27th. "Not getting the outcome is different from not having received it yet" and "Win means you win the raffle" read awkwardly.

**What I'd do**
- Add a boxed definition near the top of the main note: "Reverse. Negate both."
- Add a worked "A and B" example.
- Add one example from an LSAT-style argument.
- Remove the "need not copy it" language and move the diagramming FAQ elsewhere.
- Add the flashcards afterward.

Should I go ahead with that, or do you want me to read the four drills first so the audit is complete?

Tool result:
# Copy corrections

Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

Companion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This
says what has actually gone wrong and why.

Started 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a
correction is made.

## The rules these corrections came from

Three rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was
enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface
added later starts uncovered.

- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.
- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`
  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.
- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.

Guards now live with the content rather than with each component:
`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every
surface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.

## Corrections

### Conditional opening: define the words before using them (2026-10-03)

Joshua's marked browser paragraphs used “direction” and “guarantee” before making their meaning clear. The purpose paragraph also described an argument accepting more than its information supports without showing what that meant. Teach what a rule lets you conclude in plain language, then attach the vocabulary to a concrete relationship. Explain sufficient as enough and necessary as requir

2026-10-03 clarification: explicitly distinguish the indicator from the complete condition it refers to. Sufficient goes left; necessary goes right. The indicator itself is not a condition in the diagram. Show the same relationship with if at the beginning and in the middle, and show necessary targets both before and after requirement language. Neither sentence position nor the words immediately f

A rule describes what would follow **if** a condition were true; it does not say that condition happened. Bold that “if” and use meaningful in-view motion to expose the connection, with a static reduced-motion equivalent. Replace compressed ready-order wording with a natural full sentence. Give each paragraph and interaction a specific teaching job. These corrections supplement the canonical v

### Generic reasoning prompts → concrete numbered demonstrations (2026-09-30)

Joshua marked four parts of the V6 opening: "Read each whole idea," "Find what the statements guarantee," the abstract first sentence about information, and the paragraph combining application and linking. They named goals without showing a useful action or explanation. Replace them with numbered, separate scenarios. Show the fact and rule that prove a conclusion about one case; separately show tw

### Fact and rule definitions must explain the inference (2026-09-30)

Joshua rejected definitions that remained abstract and an explanation that did not clearly distinguish applying a rule from linking rules. Define the inputs with examples, then show the matching step and exact output. A case fact plus a rule proves something about that case. Two connecting rules produce another conditional rule without establishing a case exists. Adding a fact to the chain is a se

### Boxed `L -> R` → bold teaching notation **L → R** (2026-09-30)

Joshua rejected the recap's small monospace arrow inside a pale code box. It looked like implementation text and weakened the explanation. Use a real, clearly weighted arrow in ordinary teaching typography. Explain its complete conditions before shortening them. The Some, Most, and All reference also governs the progression: introduce the whole idea, develop the individual components, then compare

### "Concept lesson" → the actual reasoning title (2026-09-30)

The recap preview exposed an internal label above the student lesson. Joshua explicitly rejected it. Use a title such as "Facts, Rules, and What Follows" and headings that teach the reasoning. Keep production labels, version metadata, and design commentary in private records. Check the rendered HTML, since its shell can introduce wording absent from the Markdown source.

### "Recall Rephrase Colour" → "Read, Recall, Colour"
The door's heading was hardcoded to a name the product does not have, one line
under a chip reading "Read, Recall, Colour". "Rephrase" belongs to a different
Activity, Conditional Rephrase. A test asserted the wrong name, which is why
2,600 passing tests never caught it.
**Lesson:** when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that
it matches.

### "Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet…" → plain instruction
Clinical and abstract, and the sentence then told her to read "with full
intonation" while step one is silent reading. The clause that made intonation
internal, "hear it naturally in your head", had been dropped in an earlier edit.
**Lesson:** a word like "intake" reads as jargon. And when copy is trimmed,
check what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for.

### "Three beats through the stimulus" → "Three steps"
"Beat" is the internal code name for one of the four method steps. The glossary
says students see **step**. The interface ten pixels below labelled the same four
things Step 1 to Step 4, so the page used two names for one thing.
**Lesson:** never let an implementation name reach the page.

### "22 pieces to read" → line removed
A **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence, and the instructions on that same
page use the word that way. The count was of stimuli, so "22 pieces" read as 22
sentences.
**Lesson:** a unit word must mean one thing per surface. Check what a word
already means on the page before reusing it.

### "say it back" → "tell it back", "The Read" → "The Recall"
The library door still carried both retired names. "The Read" was step four's
former name and collided with step one, which is why it was renamed.
**Lesson:** a rename is not finished until every row carries it. The door was
stale for months because it is a different database row from the sections.

### "then a fourth" → "then a fourth step"
The noun sat three words back and the reader had to carry it.

### "once you have got all of it" → "once you have read all of it"
Ambiguous between having read it and having remembered it, in the one clause
that triggers step four.
**Lesson:** precision matters most where the sentence is an instruction.

### The three-fact notice → one fact
"Nothing is recording yet, the microphone opens on Start, and the section
records in one take that you cannot pause." Two of those are said again in step
four and in the long version, so she skipped the paragraph.
**Lesson:** say it once, in the place it matters.

### The method, told twice → the long version collapsed
The page rendered four numbered steps and then the whole written briefing saying
the same thing at length, both open. She read the method twice and could not
tell which one to follow.
**Lesson:** two explanations of one thing is worse than either alone.

### "PT105.S1 · 24 stimuli · about 25 min" → removed
The section was already the second line of the title. The clock is at the foot
and in the long version. The stimulus count measured the work in a unit she has
no feel for.
**Lesson:** delete a line that repeats what is above it. Do not reword it.

## Booking emails: hand over the link, not the pass settings (2026-09-08)

**Cut:** "70 minutes each, and no expiration date, so there's no rush to use them."
**Keep:** "You're set up on LSAT U with 10 sessions."

Session length and expiry are admin fields. The student sees them on the booking
page when she picks a time, so putting them in the email is detail she did not ask
for and has to read past to reach the link. The credit count is worth saying because
it tells her how much she has. Everything else about the pass stays in the backend.

**Lesson:** in a booking email, say who it's for, how many sessions, and the link.
Stop there.

## Drill hub: say what the button does, not what the feature is called (2026-09-09)

Six changes on `/classroom/drill`, from the run-3 pass. The hub named its own
features, and it assumed the student had rejected something.

| Was | Now |
|---|---|
| "Start Smart Drill" | "Practice your weakest types" |
| "Start One Passage" | "Read one passage" |
| "Start a Smart Drill instead" | "Or practice your weakest types" |
| "Read one passage instead" | "Or read one passage now" |
| "Open analytics" | "See where you lose points" |
| "Open journal" | "Review your missed questions" |

"Smart Drill" is the product's internal name for the adaptive set. On a button it
asks the student to guess what she is about to get. The sentence above the button
still names it, which is where a product name belongs: after the thing has been
explained, not as the explanation.

"Instead" was the worse one. It tells the student she has just declined the
assigned work by reading past it. She has not. "Or" offers a second path without
narrating a decision she never made.

"Open analytics" and "Open journal" name a screen. A student does not want
analytics, she wants to know where the points are going.

The fallback paragraph lost "an adaptive set that follows your weakest question
types" for "the question types you miss most": the same fact, without the product
vocabulary.

**Lesson:** a button says what happens next, in the student's words. Feature names
belong in the sentence that explains them, never on the control.

## Words never to use in student copy

From the glossary. Internal names may stay in code, never on the page.

| Never | Use instead |
|---|---|
| beat, beats | step |
| chunk | piece |
| intake | plain instruction |
| briefing, phase, tab, card | the thing's own name |
| say it back | tell it back |
| The Read | The Recall |
| Recall Rephrase Colour | Read, Recall, Colour |
| em dash, en dash | a full stop, or a colon before a list |
| expression ("Which expression identifies...") | "indicators" for the taught words, "phrase" for a multi-word indicator read as one unit ("read only if as one phrase"), "idea" or "condition" for what it points to. Added 2026-10-11: "expression" is vague and stiff; the student is looking for an indicator, so name it and reinforce the term being taught |
| statement ("Statement 1 of 7") | sentence. Added 2026-10-11: students read sentences; "statement" sounds like a logic textbook |
| Practise / "N statements" on drill cards | say what the drill has the student do. Added 2026-10-11 |
| establish ("enough to establish") | prove, or tells you. Added 2026-10-11: plainer; keep "guarantee", it is plain and sticks |
| preserve ("preserves the rule") | keeps. Added 2026-10-11 |
| relationship (when a rule is meant) | rule. Added 2026-10-11 |
| stipulated | this rule. Added 2026-10-11 |
| classify | sort. Added 2026-10-11 |
| "That keeps the meaning." on every correct step | name what was right: "Right indicator.", "Right direction."; wrong: "Not quite. Try again." Added 2026-10-11 |
| Personalized If/Then | Put yourself in it. Added 2026-10-11: names what the student does, not a feature |
| target ("the sufficient target") | what it points to, or the group. Added 2026-10-11; "target" stays in code |
| the model ("compare with the model") | our version. Added 2026-10-11 |
| "has to be true for it" (legend) | "required for the result". Added 2026-10-11: "it" had nothing to refer to; every label must make sense on its own |
| wrong-answer feedback that explains the right answer | a hint about why the chosen answer fails ("“only if” introduces what is required, not what is enough"). Added 2026-10-11: the explanation gave the answer away |
| "Build the arrow" | "Build the diagram". Added 2026-10-11: the student builds a diagram; the arrow is one symbol in it |
| "Select the words that signal the rule" | "Select the conditional indicator." Added 2026-10-11: name the thing the student is looking for |
| "28 sentences", "Sentence 1 of 28", "Next sentence" | questions: "28 questions", "Question 1 of 28", "Next question". Added 2026-10-11: a sentence is not the unit of a drill and reads unprofessional |
| "Drill 1" as a title | the task itself, e.g. "Spot the condition". Added 2026-10-11: a number says nothing about the reasoning |

**Before designing any new homework, bootcamp, drill or note, read this table.** When a
word is rejected in review, add it here first, with the reason, then fix the copy.

## Conditional Rephrase: name the student action, not the internal model (2026-09-15)

**Cut:** “record the route,” “choose the route,” and a map presented as five rules.

**Keep:** diagram the statement on paper, choose the matching diagram, and organize
the map into four groups. “Required means right” is a separate takeaway because it

# LSAT teaching: make the reasoning sound like someone thinking

**This is Joshua's selected reference for writing LSAT teaching material.** Use it for bootcamp lessons, lecture notes, explanations, guided examples, drills, flashcards, quizzes, reference pages, and instructional feedback. It develops the teaching-content register in [the canonical teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md). That document still governs correspondence and marketing in their own registers.

Joshua selected the supplied **Conditional Reasoning** transcription on 2026-10-02 because of its writing, tone, word choice, cadence, point of view, progression, and depth. The goal is to reproduce those qualities closely through original wording and examples. The central quality is patient, conversational reasoning that the reader can follow and eventually perform.

The source is a calibration example. This guide separates **observed features** from **instructions for our material**. A source habit is not automatically a rule, a research finding, or permission to import a topic. Matching the voice does not require repeating the same metaphors, anecdotes, exercise sentences, or personal claims.

## The evidence and coverage

The [source summary](../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) identifies the [preserved HTML](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html), [provenance](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/PROVENANCE.md), and SHA-256. Every supplied section, FAQ entry, table, worked diagram, and drill item was read. HTML line references below refer to that unchanged archived file.

The document has six main sections and six subordinate headings. It ends with six basic drill items and an answer-key reference. One line is explicitly marked partially obscured. The answer key is not supplied. This analysis covers the entire attachment; it cannot reconstruct the missing line or establish the contents of the larger chapter.

The HTML says its formatting was adjusted. We can examine what a pause, table, side note, or separated example does for learning. We cannot claim its font, spacing, color, or CSS exactly reproduces the original publication. No author is identified in the file. The exercise wording attributed to Shakespeare is separate from the narrator's teaching voice.

## The voice in one original passage

The following is a new calibration passage. Its example was created for this guide; it is not a source quotation or an approved bootcamp page.

> A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too.
>
> Suppose a shop has this rule: **Every parcel with a blue sticker has been inspected.**
>
> You pick up a parcel with a blue sticker. What do you know about it?
>
> It has been inspected. The rule covers every parcel with that sticker, including the one in your hand. A blue-sticker parcel that hadn't been inspected would break the rule.
>
> Now turn the parcel over. It has an inspection stamp, but you can't see a blue sticker. Does it have to have one?
>
> We don't know. The rule tells us something about parcels with blue stickers. It doesn't tell us that every inspected parcel gets a sticker.
>
> That's the distinction we need: **what the sentence makes certain, and what it leaves open.**
